<p><b>Morarji Ranchhodji Desai</b><sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> (29 February 1896 – 10 April 1995)<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> was an <a href="/wiki/Indian_independence_activist" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian independence activist">Indian independence activist</a> and politician who served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of India">4th</a> <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a> between 1977 and 1979 leading the <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Morarji_Desai" title="Premiership of Morarji Desai">government formed by the Janata Party</a>. During his long career in politics, he held many important posts in government such as <a href="/wiki/Chief_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief Minister">Chief Minister</a> of <a href="/wiki/Bombay_State" title="Bombay State">Bombay State</a>, <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of Home Affairs (India)">Home Minister</a>, <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_(India)" title="Minister of Finance (India)">Finance Minister</a> and 2nd <a href="/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Deputy Prime Minister of India">Deputy Prime Minister of India</a>.
</p><p>Following the death of Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri">Lal Bahadur Shastri</a>, Desai was a strong contender for the position of Prime Minister, only to be defeated by <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> in 1966. He was appointed as <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Finance_(India)" title="Minister of Finance (India)">Minister of Finance</a> and <a href="/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Deputy Prime Minister of India">Deputy Prime Minister</a> in Indira Gandhi's cabinet, until 1969. When Indian National Congress split in 1969 he became a part of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress_(Organisation)" title="Indian National Congress (Organisation)">INC (O)</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">controversial emergency</a> was lifted in 1977, the political parties of the opposition fought together against the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress</a> (I), under the umbrella of the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>, and won the <a href="/wiki/1977_Indian_general_election" title="1977 Indian general election">1977 election</a>. Desai was elected Prime Minister, and became the first non-Congress Prime Minister of India.
</p><p>On the international scene, Desai holds international fame for his <a href="/wiki/Peace_activist" class="mw-redirect" title="Peace activist">peace activism</a> and created efforts to initiate peace between two rival <a href="/wiki/South_Asia" title="South Asia">South Asian</a> states, Pakistan and India.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> After India's first <a href="/wiki/Smiling_Buddha" title="Smiling Buddha">nuclear test in 1974</a>, Desai helped restore friendly <a href="/wiki/China%E2%80%93India_relations" title="China–India relations">relations with China</a> and Pakistan, and vowed to avoid armed conflict such as <a href="/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_war_of_1971" class="mw-redirect" title="Indo-Pakistani war of 1971">Indo-Pakistani war of 1971</a>. He was honoured with the highest civilian award of Pakistan, the <a href="/wiki/Nishan-e-Pakistan" title="Nishan-e-Pakistan">Nishan-e-Pakistan</a> on 19 May 1990.
</p><p>He is the oldest person to hold the office of prime minister, at the age of 81, in the history of Indian politics.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> He subsequently retired from all political posts, but continued to campaign for the Janata Party in <a href="/wiki/1980_Indian_general_election" title="1980 Indian general election">1980</a>. He was conferred with India's highest civilian honour, the <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Ratna" title="Bharat Ratna">Bharat Ratna</a>. He died at the age of 99 in 1995.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Birth">Birth</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Birth">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Morarji Desai was born into a <a href="/wiki/Gujarati_people" title="Gujarati people">Gujarati</a> <a href="/wiki/Anavil_Brahmin" title="Anavil Brahmin">Anavil Brahmin</a> family.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> His father's name was Ranchhodji Nagarji Desai and his mother's name was Vajiaben Desai. He was born in Bhadeli village, <a href="/wiki/Valsad_district" title="Valsad district">Bulsar district</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bombay_Presidency" title="Bombay Presidency">Bombay Presidency</a>, <a href="/wiki/British_Raj" title="British Raj">British India</a> (present-day <a href="/wiki/Valsad_district,_Gujarat" class="mw-redirect" title="Valsad district, Gujarat">Valsad district, Gujarat</a>, India) on 29 February 1896, the eldest of eight children. His father was a school teacher.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="School_education_and_early_career">School education and early career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: School education and early career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Desai underwent his primary schooling in The Kundla School (now called J.V. Modi school), <a href="/wiki/Savarkundla" title="Savarkundla">Savarkundla</a> and later joined Bai Ava Bai High School, <a href="/wiki/Valsad" title="Valsad">Valsad</a>. Desai resigned as deputy collector of <a href="/wiki/Godhra" title="Godhra">Godhra</a> in May 1930 after being found guilty of going soft on Hindus during the riots of 1927–28 there.<sup id="cite_ref-ET-2013-06-10_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ET-2013-06-10-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Freedom_fighter">Freedom fighter</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Freedom fighter">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Desai then joined the freedom struggle under <a href="/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi" title="Mahatma Gandhi">Mahatma Gandhi</a> and joined the <a href="/wiki/Civil_disobedience" title="Civil disobedience">civil disobedience</a> movement against <a href="/wiki/British_Raj" title="British Raj">British</a> rule in India. He spent many years in jail during the <a href="/wiki/Indian_independence_movement" title="Indian independence movement">freedom struggle</a> and owing to his sharp leadership skills and tough spirit, he became a favourite amongst freedom-fighters and an important leader of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a> in the Gujarat region. When provincial elections were held in 1934 and <a href="/wiki/1937_Indian_provincial_elections" title="1937 Indian provincial elections">1937</a>, Desai was elected and served as the Revenue Minister and Home Minister of the <a href="/wiki/Bombay_Presidency" title="Bombay Presidency">Bombay Presidency</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_government">In government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: In government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Chief_Minister_of_Bombay_and_Partition_into_two_state">Chief Minister of Bombay and Partition into two state</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Chief Minister of Bombay and Partition into two state">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Desai1937.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Desai1937.jpg/220px-Desai1937.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/1a/Desai1937.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="400" /></a><figcaption>Desai in 1937, as <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress</a> Home Minister of <a href="/wiki/Bombay_Presidency" title="Bombay Presidency">Bombay Presidency</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Before the independence of India, he became Bombay's Home Minister and was later elected Chief Minister of <a href="/wiki/Bombay_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Bombay state">Bombay state</a> in 1952. It was a period when movements for linguistic states were on the rise, particularly in South India. Bombay was a bi-lingual state, home to <a href="/wiki/Gujarati_people" title="Gujarati people">Gujarati-speaking</a> and <a href="/wiki/Marathi_people" title="Marathi people">Marathi-speaking</a> people. Since 1956, activist organisation <a href="/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_Samiti" title="Samyukta Maharashtra Samiti">Samyukta Maharashtra Samiti</a> led a movement for a Marathi-speaking state of <a href="/wiki/Maharashtra" title="Maharashtra">Maharashtra</a>. Desai was opposed to such movements, including the <a href="/wiki/Mahagujarat_Movement" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahagujarat Movement">Mahagujarat Movement</a> led by <a href="/wiki/Indulal_Yagnik" title="Indulal Yagnik">Indulal Yagnik</a> demanding a new state of <a href="/wiki/Gujarat" title="Gujarat">Gujarat</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DesaiD&#39;Costa1994_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DesaiD&#39;Costa1994-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Desai proposed that the metropolitan <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a> be made a Union territory. His logic was that a separate development region would suit the city's cosmopolitan nature, with citizens from diverse settings across various linguistic, cultural, and religious backgrounds living there for generations. The movement led to violence across the city and state, and Desai ordered the police to open fire on the <a href="/wiki/Samyukta_Maharashtra_Samiti" title="Samyukta Maharashtra Samiti">Samyukta Maharashtra Samiti</a> demonstrators who had gathered at <a href="/wiki/Flora_Fountain" title="Flora Fountain">Flora Fountain</a>. The protesters were led by <a href="/wiki/Senapati_Bapat" class="mw-redirect" title="Senapati Bapat">Senapati Bapat</a>. In the carnage that followed, 105 protesters, were killed. The issue escalated and is believed to have forced the Central Government to agree to two separate states based on language. After the formation of the present State of Maharashtra, Bombay, now <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a> became its state capital. Flora Fountain was renamed  "<a href="/wiki/Hutatma_Chowk" title="Hutatma Chowk">Hutatma Chowk</a>" ("Martyrs' Square" in English) to honour the people killed in the firing. Later Desai moved to Delhi when he was inducted as finance Minister in the cabinet of Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nehru_cabinet">Nehru cabinet</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Nehru cabinet">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Desai was socially conservative, pro-business, and in favour of free enterprise reforms, as opposed to Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a>'s socialistic policies.
</p><p>Rising in Congress leadership, as a fierce nationalist with anti-corruption leanings, Desai was at odds with Prime Minister Nehru and his allies, and with Nehru's age and health failing, he was considered as a possible contender for the position of Prime Minister.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Congress_party_leadership_contest">Congress party leadership contest</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Congress party leadership contest">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1964 after prime minister's Nehru's death, Desai was outflanked in the leadership contest by the Nehru's protege, <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri">Lal Bahadur Shastri</a>. Desai was invited but did not join the short lived Shastri cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-HaqqiAssociation1986_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HaqqiAssociation1986-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
In early 1966, the unexpected passing away of Prime Minister Lal Bahadur Shastri after only 18 months in power made Desai once again a contender for the top position. However, he was defeated by Nehru's daughter, <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>, in the Congress party leadership election by a big margin.<sup id="cite_ref-Singh1981a_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Singh1981a-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Indira_Gandhi_cabinet">Indira Gandhi cabinet</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Indira Gandhi cabinet">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Desai served as Deputy Prime Minister and Finance Minister of India in the Indira Gandhi government until July 1969 when Prime Minister Gandhi took the finance portfolio from him but asked him to serve as the deputy prime minister. However, to save his self-respect, Desai tendered his resignation from the Gandhi cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-Singh1981b_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Singh1981b-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Gandhi also nationalized the fourteen largest banks in India at the same time.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_opposition">In opposition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: In opposition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>When the Congress party split in 1969, Desai joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress_(Organisation)" title="Indian National Congress (Organisation)">Indian National Congress (Organisation)</a> faction of the party, whereas Indira Gandhi formed a new faction called Indian National Congress (Requisitionists). Alternatively, the two factions of Desai and Indira were called Syndicate and Indicate respectively. The 1971 general elections to the Indian parliament were won by Indira Gandhi's faction in a landslide. Desai, however, was elected as a member of the Lok Sabha or lower house of Parliament. Desai went on indefinite hunger strike on 12 March 1975 to support <a href="/wiki/Nav_Nirman" class="mw-redirect" title="Nav Nirman">Nav Nirman</a> movement of Gujarat.<sup id="cite_ref-avk_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-avk-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1975, Indira Gandhi was convicted of electoral fraud by the Allahabad High Court, after opponents alleged she had used government civil servants and equipment during the campaign for the 1971 General Elections.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> During the subsequent Emergency rule in 1975–77, Desai and other opposition leaders were jailed by the Indira Gandhi government as part of a massive crackdown.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Janata_wave_of_1977">Janata wave of 1977</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Janata wave of 1977">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The popular anti-corruption movement led by <a href="/wiki/Jayaprakash_Narayan" title="Jayaprakash Narayan">Jayaprakash Narayan</a> and the Janata-wave in 1977 led to the complete routing of the Congress party in Northern India, and a landslide victory for the opposition Janata alliance in the National elections held in March 1977. Desai was selected by the Janata alliance, later <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a> as their parliamentary leader, and thus became the first non-Congress Prime Minister of India.<sup id="cite_ref-Mirchandani2003_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mirchandani2003-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_Minister_of_India_.281977-1979.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister_of_India_(1977-1979)">Prime Minister of India (1977-1979)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Prime Minister of India (1977-1979)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="As_a_Prime_Minister">As a Prime Minister</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: As a Prime Minister">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In January 1977, Indira Gandhi dissolved the Lok Sabha and declared that elections to the body were to be held during March 1977. Opposition leaders were also released and promptly formed the Janata alliance to fight the elections. The alliance registered a landslide victory in the election. On the urging of <a href="/wiki/Jayaprakash_Narayan" title="Jayaprakash Narayan">Jayaprakash Narayan</a>, the Janata alliance selected Desai as their parliamentary leader and thus the Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-Mirchandani2003_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mirchandani2003-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policy">Foreign policy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Foreign policy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Desai restored normal relations with China, for the first time since <a href="/wiki/Sino-Indian_War" title="Sino-Indian War">the 1962 war</a>. He also communicated with the military ruler of Pakistan, General <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Zia-ul-Haq" title="Muhammad Zia-ul-Haq">Zia-ul-Haq</a> and established friendly relations. Despite his pacifist leanings, he refused to sign the non-nuclear proliferation treaty despite the threat of stopping supply of uranium for power plants by the USA Congress.<sup id="cite_ref-Alam1988_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Alam1988-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nuclear_programme">Nuclear programme</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Nuclear programme">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Domestically, Desai played a crucial role in the <a href="/wiki/Indian_nuclear_programme" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian nuclear programme">Indian nuclear program</a> after it was targeted by major nuclear powers after India conducted a <a href="/wiki/Smiling_Buddha" title="Smiling Buddha">surprise nuclear test in 1974</a>. Desai kept India's nuclear reactors stating "they will never be used for atomic bombs, and I will see to it if I can help it".<sup id="cite_ref-The_World:_Morarji_Desai:_The_Ascetic_Activist_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_World:_Morarji_Desai:_The_Ascetic_Activist-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Internationally, he reaffirmed India's stand that it would not manufacture nuclear weapons and would refrain from conducting even peaceful nuclear explosions.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> In 1977, the <a href="/wiki/Presidency_of_Jimmy_Carter" title="Presidency of Jimmy Carter">Carter administration</a> offered to sell <a href="/wiki/Heavy_water" title="Heavy water">heavy water</a> and <a href="/wiki/Uranium" title="Uranium">uranium</a> to India for its nuclear reactors but required American on-site inspection of nuclear materials. Desai declined, seeing the American stance as contradictory, in light of its own nuclear arsenal.<sup id="cite_ref-Nation:_Jimmy&#39;s_Journey:_Mostly_Pluses_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nation:_Jimmy&#39;s_Journey:_Mostly_Pluses-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Decimation_of_R.26AW"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Decimation_of_R&amp;AW">Decimation of R&amp;AW</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Decimation of R&amp;AW">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Desai had described the <a href="/wiki/Research_and_Analysis_Wing" title="Research and Analysis Wing">Research and Analysis Wing</a> (R&amp;AW), India's external intelligence agency, as the <a href="/wiki/Praetorian_guard" class="mw-redirect" title="Praetorian guard">praetorian guard</a> of <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> and had promised to stop all activities of the R&amp;AW after becoming prime minister. He closed down much of the agency, and reduced its budget and operations,<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> such as closing its Information Division.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/B._Raman" title="B. Raman">B. Raman</a>, the former head of the Counter-Terrorism Division of R&amp;AW and noted security analyst, revealed that in an informal discussion, Desai indiscreetly told Pakistan's <a href="/wiki/Chief_Martial_Law_Administrator" title="Chief Martial Law Administrator">Chief Martial Law Administrator</a> General <a href="/wiki/General_Zia-ul-Haq" class="mw-redirect" title="General Zia-ul-Haq">Zia ul-Haq</a> that his government was well aware of Pakistan's <a href="/wiki/Pakistan_and_its_Nuclear_Deterrent_Program" class="mw-redirect" title="Pakistan and its Nuclear Deterrent Program">nuclear development</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Intra-party_squabbles_and_collapse_of_Janata_government">Intra-party squabbles and collapse of Janata government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Intra-party squabbles and collapse of Janata government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>His government undid many <a href="/wiki/Forty-second_Amendment_of_the_Constitution_of_India" title="Forty-second Amendment of the Constitution of India">amendments made to the constitution during emergency</a> and made it more difficult for any future government to impose a national emergency.  However, the Janata Party coalition was full of personal and policy friction and thus failed to achieve much, owing to continuous in-wrangling and much controversy.<sup id="cite_ref-Singh1981c_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Singh1981c-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> With no party in leadership of the coalition, rival groups vied to unseat Desai.  Controversial trials of prominent Congress leaders, including <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> over Emergency-era abuses worsened the fortunes of his administration.
In 1979, <a href="/wiki/Raj_Narain" title="Raj Narain">Raj Narain</a> and <a href="/wiki/Choudhary_Charan_Singh" class="mw-redirect" title="Choudhary Charan Singh">Charan Singh</a> pulled out of the Janata Party, forcing Desai to resign from office and retire from politics.  The chief reason for the collapse was the demand by the duo and other left-leaning members, like <a href="/wiki/Madhu_Limaye" title="Madhu Limaye">Madhu Limaye</a>, <a href="/wiki/Krishan_Kant" title="Krishan Kant">Krishan Kant</a>, and <a href="/wiki/George_Fernandes" title="George Fernandes">George Fernandes</a> that no member of the Janata party could simultaneously be a member of an alternative social or political organisation.  This attack on "dual membership" was directed specifically at members of the Janata Party who had been members of the <a href="/wiki/Jan_Sangh" class="mw-redirect" title="Jan Sangh">Jan Sangh</a>, and continued to be members of <a href="/wiki/Rashtriya_Swayamsevak_Sangh" title="Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh">Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh</a>, the Jan Sangh's ideological parent.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Retirement_and_death">Retirement and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Retirement and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Desai campaigned for the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a> in <a href="/wiki/1980_Indian_general_election" title="1980 Indian general election">1980 General Election</a> as a senior politician but did not contest the election himself. In retirement, he lived in <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a>. When former French <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of France">Prime Minister</a> <a href="/wiki/Antoine_Pinay" title="Antoine Pinay">Antoine Pinay</a> died on 13 December 1994, Desai became the world's oldest living former head of government. He was much honoured in his last years as a freedom-fighter of his generation. On his 99th birthday, he was visited by Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao" title="P. V. Narasimha Rao">P. V. Narasimha Rao</a>, and soon after began to fall ill.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> He was treated in a hospital in Mumbai due to low blood pressure and a chest infection. He died on 10 April 1995, aged 99, after he underwent surgery for a blood clot in his brain.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Desai was a <a href="/wiki/Morality" title="Morality">moralist</a>. He was a <a href="/wiki/Vegetarians" class="mw-redirect" title="Vegetarians">vegetarian</a> "both by birth and by conviction."<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_service">Social service</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Social service">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Desai was a Gandhian follower, social worker, institution builder and a great reformer. He was the Chancellor of Gujarat Vidyapith. Even during his term as the Prime Minister he used to visit and stay at Vidyapith during the month of October. He lived simply and used to write post cards himself even when he held the office of Prime Minister. Sardar Patel deputed him to conduct meetings of farmers in Kaira district which finally led to the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Amul" title="Amul">Amul</a> Cooperative movement. During his rule, he withdrew intervention in <a href="/wiki/Public_Distribution_System" class="mw-redirect" title="Public Distribution System">Public Distribution System</a> and rationing shops were literally lost due to cheap sugar and oil available in the market.
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Desai married Gujraben in 1911, at the age of 15.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Gujraben lived to see her husband becoming Prime Minister. She died on 25 October 1981 at the age of 81.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Out of his five children, only three survived infancy. Desai's three surviving children were: his daughters Virumati, Indu and his son Kantilal.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Virumati, who was married to Ramanlal Desai, died in early 2000s. Indu, a medical student died in 1953. Kantilal Desai, who was married to Padma Kirloskar, died in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-auto1_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Desai has two grandsons, through his son Kantilal; Bharat Desai and Jagdeep Desai, and one granddaughter; Varsha Desai Naik. One of Desai's great-grandsons, <a href="/wiki/Madhukeshwar_Desai" title="Madhukeshwar Desai">Madhukeshwar Desai</a> is currently the National Vice-President of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Yuva_Morcha" title="Bharatiya Janata Yuva Morcha">Bharatiya Janata Yuva Morcha</a>, the youth wing of the BJP.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Madhukeshwar is married to Sneha Menon, a celebrity talkshow host. Of Desai's great-granddaughters; Kalayani is a celebrity stylist and designer, Disha is a Harvard-graduate Lawyer and Sanghavi is a social worker and benefactor for several noted charities.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Another of Desai's great-grandsons, Vishaal Desai is a writer and filmmaker.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Desai, a longtime practitioner of '<a href="/wiki/Urine_therapy" title="Urine therapy">urine therapy</a>', spoke in 1978 to <a href="/wiki/Dan_Rather" title="Dan Rather">Dan Rather</a> on <i><a href="/wiki/60_Minutes" title="60 Minutes">60 Minutes</a></i> about the benefits of drinking urine.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> He also attributed his longevity to drinking urine.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_popular_culture">In popular culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: In popular culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><i>Morarji</i>, an Indian television series about the life of Desai from his days as an independence activist till his tenure as prime minister, aired on the national public broadcaster <a href="/wiki/Doordarshan" title="Doordarshan">Doordarshan</a>'s <a href="/wiki/DD_National" title="DD National">DD National</a> channel.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Films_Division_of_India" title="Films Division of India">Films Division of India</a> made <i>For Peace and Friendship</i>, a 1978 <a href="/wiki/Short_film" title="Short film">short</a> <a href="/wiki/Documentary_film" title="Documentary film">documentary film</a> directed by C. Ramani about Desai's campaign for nuclear disarmament.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Govind_Namdeo" class="mw-redirect" title="Govind Namdeo">Govind Namdeo</a> appeared as Desai in the 2019 <a href="/wiki/Conspiracy_thriller" class="mw-redirect" title="Conspiracy thriller">conspiracy thriller</a> film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Gandhi_Murder" title="The Gandhi Murder">The Gandhi Murder</a></i>, by Karim Traïdia and Pankaj Sehgal, which explores a British abetment in the assassination of Mahatma Gandhi.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Avijit_Dutt" title="Avijit Dutt">Avijit Dutt</a> appears as Desai in <a href="/wiki/Mission_Majnu" title="Mission Majnu">Mission Majnu</a>.
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Pradhanmantri" title="Pradhanmantri">Pradhanmantri</a></i> (<abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Prime Minister&#39;), a 2013 Indian docudrama television series which aired on <a href="/wiki/ABP_News" title="ABP News">ABP News</a> and covers the various policies and political tenures of Indian PMs, dedicated the thirteenth episode – "Story of Morarji Desai and Janata Party" – to his term as the country's leader.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Morarji_Desai&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
